Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo test -p xtask -- --nocapture` (`47/47`), `cargo xtask summarize-compat --locked` (`7/7`), `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-dom` (`133/133`; generated coverage remains `rust-backed 34/34`, `mixed 99/99`, `shim-backed 0/0`), `cargo xtask run-option-matrix --all` (`7/7`), `cargo xtask run-output-contract --all` (`7/7`), and `git diff --check`.
- [x] SFC/style source-map traceability slice: `vuec_style` now emits line-level source maps with real `sourcesContent`, original file ids, and base offsets; `vuec_sfc` now merges multiple style blocks into one `.vue` source map without duplicate sources and skips empty style blocks when advancing generated mappings. The Node bridge Vue 2.7 direct `compileStyle` path forwards the original source-map metadata. This is Rust SFC/style behavior only and does not claim global source-map parity.
- [x] Rust style CSS Modules slice: `vuec_style` now rewrites module class selectors and returns optional module export maps for default local scoping, `:global(...)`, `:local(...)`, `scopeBehaviour: "global"`, scoped-name templates, and `localsConvention: "camelCaseOnly"`. SFC, node bridge, NAPI, WASM, and direct JS loaders now pass `modules` / `modulesOptions` into Rust. Generated and NAPI SFC option/output/conformance gates remain green, while Vue 3 SFC official conformance stays reported as mixed coverage rather than pure Rust-backed SFC parity.
- [x] CSS Modules locals-convention option slice: `vuec_style` now distinguishes `localsConvention: "dashes"` and `"dashesOnly"` from camel-case conventions, matching `postcss-modules` dash-only key generation while preserving underscore-delimited names as distinct exports. `vuec_sfc` forwards the option through the public style wrapper. This extends the Rust CSS Modules option surface but does not close the full `postcss-modules` option matrix.
- [x] CSS Modules `globalModulePaths` option slice: `vuec_style` now accepts static string patterns in `modulesOptions.globalModulePaths` / `global_module_paths`, matches them against the entry CSS filename, and uses the resulting default global scope for the entry file and its loader dependencies while preserving explicit `:local(...)` exports. This is Rust style/SFC option behavior; RegExp object transport, invalid-pattern error parity, JavaScript resolver/loader callbacks, and full PostCSS Modules option parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules scoped-name hash slice: `vuec_style` now matches official CSS Modules default scoped-name hashing/line-number output and supports `modulesOptions.hashPrefix` for `generateScopedName` templates backed by the official `xxhash64` hash content shape. `vuec_sfc` forwards the option through style compilation. Function-valued `generateScopedName`, JS resolver/loader behavior, and full PostCSS Modules parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules class attribute selector parity slice: `vuec_style` now locks the official public behavior that `[class="btn"]`, `[class='btn']`, `[class=btn]`, and `[class~=btn]` selectors remain unlocalized in CSS Modules mode, even inside `:local(...)`, while ordinary `.btn` selectors in the same file still scope/export normally. This is Rust style/SFC behavior and does not add alias shim semantics.
- [x] CSS Modules ID selector slice: `vuec_style` now scopes local ID selectors in CSS Modules mode, exports their scoped names, preserves class/id compounds such as `.button#item`, respects `scopeBehaviour: "global"` with explicit `:local(#id)`, and exports global IDs when `modulesOptions.exportGlobals` is enabled. JS resolver/loader behavior and full PostCSS Modules parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules keyframes/animation slice: `vuec_style` now scopes local `@keyframes` names, exports keyframe tokens, rewrites `animation-name` and `animation` declaration values with official animation-keyword handling, and respects `scopeBehaviour: "global"` / `@keyframes :local(...)` boundaries. `vuec_sfc` forwards the behavior through public style compilation. Full PostCSS Modules diagnostics and JS resolver/loader behavior remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules `exportGlobals` option slice: `vuec_style` now includes global class selectors in the module export map when `modulesOptions.exportGlobals` is enabled, without rewriting those selectors, and applies the same locals-convention reducer to global exports. `vuec_sfc` forwards the option through the public style wrapper. This extends Rust CSS Modules parity but leaves callback/import-loader ecosystem behavior open.
- [x] CSS vars comment-separated binding slice: `vuec_style` now collects and rewrites official comment-separated `v-bind /*...*/ (expr)` CSS variable bindings with distinct collect/rewrite boundaries, keeps the no-whitespace `v-bind/**/(expr)` compileStyle rewrite boundary, and forwards the behavior through SFC style and Vue 2.7 parse bridge paths; the same round fixed scoped deep ordinary declaration formatting without changing nested deep passthrough semantics.
- [x] CSS Modules same-file `composes` slice: `vuec_style` now stores raw module exports before final locals-convention projection, supports `composes` / `compose-with` for previously defined same-file local classes and `global(...)` class names, removes handled compose declarations from generated CSS, and exposes composed values through SFC style results. External `from` loader composition and official invalid-compose diagnostics remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules ICSS `:export` slice: `vuec_style` now removes local `:export` rules from generated CSS and adds their declarations to the module export map before final locals-convention projection. This covers local ICSS exports only; `:import(...)` symbol replacement and external loader resolution remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules relative import slice: `vuec_style` now loads local relative CSS Modules dependencies for `:import("./dep.css")` symbol replacement and `composes: dep from "./dep.css"` / multi-class external composition, prepends compiled dependency CSS once, and threads the behavior through the SFC style wrapper. JS `resolve` / `Loader` callbacks, `node_modules` lookup, and full PostCSS Modules loader parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules node_modules loader slice: `vuec_style` now resolves bare CSS Modules imports through a Rust default file-system loader path that walks ancestor `node_modules` directories and supports package subpaths plus `package.json` `main` CSS entries for `:import("pkg")` and `composes: dep from "pkg/file.css"`. This does not implement JavaScript `resolve` / `Loader` callbacks, package `exports` conditions, or full PostCSS Modules loader parity.
- [x] CSS Modules package exports loader slice: `vuec_style` now resolves static package `exports` CSS entries for bare package roots and exact subpaths, including `exports: "./theme.css"` and `{ "./theme.css": "./dist/theme.css" }`, while keeping unexported subpaths blocked instead of falling back to physical files. Conditional exports, wildcard exports, JavaScript resolver callbacks, and full Node loader parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules advanced package exports loader slice: `vuec_style` now resolves static `default` condition objects for package export roots and subpaths plus simple wildcard subpath patterns such as `{ "./*.css": "./dist/*.css" }` in the Rust default CSS Modules file-system loader. Non-`default` condition priority, complex package `exports` pattern semantics, JavaScript resolver callbacks, and full Node loader parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules CJS package exports condition slice: `vuec_style` now parses package `exports` objects without losing key order and resolves static CJS-active condition objects using `require` / `node` / `default`, including nested condition objects for package roots and exact subpaths. This improves Rust default loader parity for CSS Modules package lookup, while the full Node condition set, custom conditions, complex pattern semantics, JavaScript resolver callbacks, and full Node loader parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules `from global` composition slice: `vuec_style` now supports official `composes: reset utility from global` syntax, appending those global class names to the local module export while removing the compose declaration from generated CSS. This is Rust style/SFC behavior and does not add alias shim semantics.
- [x] CSS Modules compose diagnostics slice: `vuec_style` now reports structured `VUEC_STYLE_MODULE_COMPOSE` diagnostics for same-file and declaration-order invalid `composes` class references, maps spans back through SFC style offsets, and preserves official external missing-class behavior as `undefined` composition. Full PostCSS Modules diagnostics remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules complex selector compose diagnostics slice: `vuec_style` now reports `VUEC_STYLE_MODULE_COMPOSE` for `composes` declarations on selectors that are not a single local class selector, removes the invalid declaration from generated CSS, and forwards the public error through `vuec_sfc` with official-prefix message shape. JS resolver/loader behavior and full PostCSS Modules diagnostic parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules local `@value` slice: `vuec_style` now removes/exports local `@value name: value;` declarations and applies official single-pass value replacement in selectors, at-rule preludes, declarations, and strings while skipping comments. `vuec_sfc` forwards this through public style compilation. `@value ... from` imports, JavaScript resolver/Loader callbacks, and full PostCSS Modules parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules static `@value ... from` import slice: `vuec_style` now resolves static value imports through the Rust CSS Modules file loader, exports imported values, replaces imported value/class tokens in declarations, at-rule preludes, selectors, strings, and `composes`, preserves imported class values without re-scoping, and follows the official missing-import `undefined` / `i__const_*` shape. `vuec_sfc` forwards this through public style compilation. JS resolver/Loader callbacks and full PostCSS Modules parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules static `@value` missing compose slice: `vuec_style` now distinguishes imported value CSS replacement from module/compose value, so missing static value imports keep the official `i__const_*` CSS replacement while `composes` appends `undefined`. `vuec_sfc` forwards this through public style compilation. JavaScript resolver/Loader callbacks and full PostCSS Modules parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules imported CSS formatting slice: `vuec_style` now normalizes dependency CSS through the entry style normalization path while keeping original dependency source for scoped-name hashes, and preserves official block-boundary newline behavior for recursive static value imports. This closes the previous static `@value ... from` imported-CSS formatting caveat without adding alias shim semantics.
- [x] CSS Modules ICSS import symbol replacement slice: `vuec_style` now applies `:import(...)` symbol replacements across selectors, at-rule preludes, quoted strings, declaration values, and `composes`, avoids exporting or re-scoping imported selector tokens as local module keys, and removes the blank whitespace left by stripped ICSS rules. `vuec_sfc` forwards this through public style compilation. JavaScript resolver/Loader callbacks and full PostCSS Modules parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules ICSS missing import/export alias slice: `vuec_style` now preserves official missing `:import(...)` symbol semantics: missing selector tokens stay global and unexported, declarations / at-rules / strings stay unreplaced, `composes` and `:export` aliases receive `undefined`, and found `:export` import aliases resolve to the imported value. `vuec_sfc` also preserves `modules: {}` when CSS Modules succeeds with no exports. JavaScript resolver/Loader callbacks and full PostCSS Modules parity remain open.
- [x] CSS Modules native nesting rewrite slice: `vuec_style` now recursively rewrites CSS Modules selectors inside native nested rule bodies, including direct nested selectors, `&` selectors, nested `@media` / `@supports`, and nested `:local(...)` / `:global(...)`, while preserving declaration order around nested blocks. `vuec_sfc` forwards this through public style compilation. - [x] CSS Modules native nested compose diagnostics slice: `vuec_style` now treats `composes` / `compose-with` inside native nested style rules as the official fatal CSS Modules error, normalizes nested `from global` messages to `global(...)`, and returns empty generated CSS plus no module map for fatal compose diagnostics. Top-level style rules inside container at-rules still allow valid composition. `vuec_sfc` forwards this through public style compilation. JavaScript resolver/Loader callbacks and full PostCSS Modules parity remain open.
- [x] Rust style error-mapping slice: `vuec_style` now emits structured diagnostics for current Rust style errors while preserving string `errors`; unresolved `@import` statements and unsupported preprocessors carry source spans, `vuec_sfc` maps those spans back to the original `.vue` offsets, and CLI/API callers can consume the structured diagnostics. This does not claim full PostCSS/lightningcss diagnostic parity.
- [x] Rust scoped `:slotted()` selector rewrite slice: `vuec_style` now preserves selector prefix/suffix context for top-level `:slotted(...)` / `::v-slotted(...)`, injects the official `[scope-s]` slot marker into the inner selector, leaves nested `:not(:slotted(...))` style pseudos on the normal scoped path, and aligns universal selector scope targets with official `compileStyle` output.
- [x] Rust scoped `:slotted()` universal-leading-combinator slice: `vuec_style` now matches official no-prefix `:slotted(* + .foo)` output by trimming only the leading whitespace left after universal selector removal, including inside top-level `:is()` / `:where()`, while preserving official prefixed selector spacing such as `.a :slotted(* + .foo)`. `vuec_sfc` forwards the behavior through public style compilation. - [x] Rust scoped escaped selector token slice: `vuec_style` now consumes CSS escapes while scanning selector lists, pseudo functions, parens/brackets, deep combinators, and scope-injection tokens, so escaped `:` / `,`, escaped pseudo-like class names, CSS hex escapes, and non-ASCII identifier tokens receive scope injection at the official location. `vuec_sfc` forwards the behavior through public style compilation. - [x] Rust scoped selector comment slice: `vuec_style` now skips `/*...*/` comments during selector-list, pseudo, bracket, deep-combinator, and injection-target scanning, removes comments that official treats as whitespace/combinator trivia, preserves compound selector comments such as `.foo/*x*/.bar`, and scopes preserved comment suffixes like `.foo/*x*/:hover` at the official position. `vuec_sfc` forwards the behavior through public style compilation. - [x] Vue 2 compiler Rust codegen/data-object parity slice for `ref`, `refInFor`, and `v-model`, fixing plain-element data generation, duplicate model runtime directive output, model assignment whitespace preservation, and JS expression-valued DOM prop newline handling; current official results improve to Vue 2.6 compiler `64/188` and Vue 2.7 compiler `62/190`, while Vue 2.7 SFC remains `5/144`.
- [x] Vue 2 compiler Rust event-handler codegen parity slice, preserving event modifier source order, matching official handler wrapping for event-option modifiers, and aligning method call / inline statement classification; current official results improve to Vue 2.6 compiler `72/188` and Vue 2.7 compiler `70/190`, while Vue 2.7 SFC remains `5/144`.
- [x] Vue 2 compiler Rust scoped-slot codegen parity slice, cleaning scoped-slot children from normal child output, emitting scoped-slot data objects, and supporting dynamic/conditional legacy and new slot syntax codegen; current official results improve to Vue 2.6 compiler `77/188` and Vue 2.7 compiler `75/190`, while Vue 2.7 SFC remains `5/144`.
- [x] Vue 2 compiler Rust inline-template codegen and warning parity slice, generating inline render/staticRenderFns from the first child element and forwarding Rust compiler warnings through the Vue 2 official warning channel adapter; current official results improve to Vue 2.6 compiler `95/188` and Vue 2.7 compiler `92/190`, while Vue 2.7 SFC remains `5/144`.
- [x] Vue 2 compiler Rust `v-pre` template codegen parity slice, preserving descendant literal text and rendering `<template>` as a real element inside `v-pre`; current official results improve to Vue 2.6 compiler `96/188` and Vue 2.7 compiler `93/190`, while Vue 2.7 SFC remains `5/144`.
- [x] Vue 2 compiler Rust codeframe parity slice, matching official multiline highlight window behavior; current official results improve to Vue 2.6 compiler `97/188`, while Vue 2.7 compiler remains `93/190` and Vue 2.7 SFC remains `5/144`.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust-backed `cssVars` slice, covering CSS `v-bind()` parsing/rewrite, dev/prod variable naming, normal script injection, script setup injection, and binding metadata (`cssVars.spec.ts` `13/13`; full `vue27-sfc` currently `48/144`, all `rust-backed`).
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust `compileScript` default-export wrapper / auto name inference slice, rewriting normal script default exports to `__default__`, preserving manual `name`, inferring filename `__name`, and emitting official JS/TS wrapper shapes; focused official `compileScript.spec.ts -t "auto name inference"` passes `5/5`, focused binding analysis now passes `18/18`, full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance improves to `80/144` as `rust-backed`, and remaining compileScript gaps are defineProps/defineEmits/defineExpose codegen, TS macro/type handling, type hoisting, diagnostics, import/template usage filtering, and return-object parity.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust `compileScript` runtime macro slice, generating runtime `props:` / `emits:` options, setup context params for bound emits/expose, props aliases, `defineExpose` rewrites, and multi-declaration macro removal for `defineProps` / `defineEmits` / `defineExpose`; focused official runtime macro subset passes `10/10`, full `compileScript.spec.ts` improves to `35/77`, and full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance improves to `92/144` as `rust-backed`.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust `compileScript` return binding / template import-usage slice, returning normal-script declarations, setup declarations, and template-used imports in official order while excluding type-only imports; focused official top-level declarations / dev-mode import usage / import-type subset passes `10/10`, full `compileScript.spec.ts` improves to `45/77`, and full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance improves to `102/144` as `rust-backed`.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust `compileScript` TypeScript declaration hoist / runtime enum slice, moving setup TS declarations and runtime enums to module scope with official whitespace, returning setup enums, and preserving normal-script enum return order; focused official TS hoist/enum subset passes `4/4`, full `compileScript.spec.ts` improves to `49/77`, and full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance improves to `106/144` as `rust-backed`.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust `compileScript` TypeScript macro inference slice, resolving `defineProps<T>()`, `withDefaults(...)`, and `defineEmits<T>()` from inline and referenced TS declarations, generating runtime props/emits/defaults and unsupported emits-type errors in Rust; focused official TS macro subset passes `17/17`, full `compileScript.spec.ts` improves to `66/77`, and full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance improves to `123/144` as `rust-backed`.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust `compileScript` import merge / macro diagnostics slice, deduping normal/setup imports, hoisting setup side-effect imports with comments, preserving script block module order, reporting official macro/co-usage diagnostics, and cleaning named default export-only normal scripts; full `compileScript.spec.ts` now passes `77/77`, and full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance improves to `134/144` as `rust-backed`.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust `compileTemplate` template preprocessor slice, adding Rust SFC preprocessing for Pug/Jade indentation templates plus official missing-preprocessor errors/tips; full `compileTemplate.spec.ts` now passes `9/9`, and full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance improves to `136/144` as `rust-backed`.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC Rust `compileStyle` CSS preprocessor slice, adding Rust Less/SCSS/Sass/Stylus preprocessing before scoped/CSS-var transforms and preserving source maps for preprocessed styles; focused official `compileStyle.spec.ts` improves to `6/10`, and full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance improves to `140/144` as `rust-backed`.
- [x] Vue 2.7 SFC `compileStyle` PostCSS API adapter slice, executing caller-provided PostCSS plugin callbacks/options in the generated JavaScript alias after Rust style compilation because callbacks cannot cross the JSON bridge; focused official `compileStyle.spec.ts` now passes `10/10`, and full Vue 2.7 SFC conformance passes `144/144` with coverage `mixed` (`rust-backed 134/134`, `mixed 10/10`).

## Current Release Documentation Slice

- Reran the remaining M20 release verification gates. `cargo xtask verify-release-dry-run` reports `25` total, `5` pass, `20` pending, and `0` fail; `cargo xtask verify-release-install-smoke` reports `9` total, `2` pass, `7` pending, and `0` fail.
- Recorded the remaining objective blockers in `docs/UNRESOLVED_PROBLEMS.md`: non-current native platform npm packages need matching release-built `vuec_napi.node` artifacts and host/CI smoke runs, and first-time crates.io dry-runs for crates with internal path dependencies require ordered dependency publication to the registry.
- Updated `docs/2.DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md` to mark the M20 release dry-run and install-smoke items complete for local repository work. The gates still report `pending`, not `pass`, so release readiness remains auditable.
- This slice is release verification/documentation only. It does not change compiler semantics, `xtask/src/compat.rs`, conformance classification, or AST/HIR/MIR structures.
- Expanded the M20 rustdoc-enforced public API documentation gate to the remaining runner/CLI support crates. `vuec_cli`, `vuec_node_bridge`, and `xtask` binary crates now have crate-level docs, with `vuec_node_bridge` explicitly classified as API/import adapter and test-runner support rather than compiler semantics and `xtask` classified as verification automation. `vuec_runtime_tests` now denies missing docs and documents runtime version selection, smoke fixtures/results, and mount/render/SSR/hydration helper APIs.
- `cargo xtask verify-public-api-docs` now covers all twenty current workspace packages: `vuec_source`, `vuec_diagnostics`, `vuec_codegen`, `vuec_ast`, `vuec_html`, `vuec_js`, `vuec_pass`, `vuec_style`, `vuec_vue3_asset`, `vuec_vue3_core`, `vuec_vue3_dom`, `vuec_vue3_ssr`, `vuec_vue2`, `vuec_sfc`, `vuec_napi`, `vuec_wasm`, `vuec_node_bridge`, `vuec_runtime_tests`, `vuec_cli`, and `xtask`.

- Added the first M20 rustdoc-enforced public API documentation slice. `vuec_source`, `vuec_diagnostics`, and `vuec_codegen` now deny missing docs at crate level, and `cargo xtask verify-public-api-docs` runs `cargo doc --no-deps` with `RUSTDOCFLAGS=-D missing_docs` for those crates.

- Added the M20 release install-smoke gate. `cargo xtask verify-release-install-smoke` now creates tarballs from release-built packages, installs them into clean temporary npm projects, verifies `@vuec-rs/native` loads through the current optional platform package, and verifies `@vuec-rs/wasm` initializes through the package default entry after install.
- Fixed the published WASM package loader so Node defaults to `pkg-node/vuec_wasm.js` while browser-like runtimes still default to `pkg/vuec_wasm.js`. This fixes installed-package behavior, not compiler semantics.
- Current install-smoke status is honest `pending`, not complete: the current Windows package and WASM package pass, while non-current native platform package install smokes require matching target-platform release artifacts and host runs. This is recorded in `docs/PENDING_DECISIONS.md`; the M20 completion checkbox for published artifact install smoke remains open.
- Added the M20 release dry-run gate. `cargo xtask verify-release-dry-run` now stages release package trees under `target/release-dry-run`, builds release NAPI/WASM artifacts, runs `npm pack --dry-run --json` for `@vuec-rs/native`, `@vuec-rs/wasm`, and the current native platform package, verifies required tarball file entries, and runs Cargo dry-run checks for publishable crates where crates.io can resolve dependencies.
- Current dry-run status is honest `pending`, not complete: on this Windows host, non-current native platform packages cannot prove tarball contents without their own release-built `vuec_napi.node` artifacts, and first-time crates.io dry-runs for crates with internal path dependencies remain pending until dependency crates are published in release order. This is recorded in `docs/PENDING_DECISIONS.md`; the M20 completion checkbox for release dry-run remains open.
- Added the M20 release-facing API documentation gate. `docs/API.md` now documents supported Rust crate entry points, `vuec` commands, `@vuec-rs/native` exports, `@vuec-rs/wasm` exports, official package-name aliases, and API verification commands.
- Extended `cargo xtask verify-release-docs` so the API document is checked for the required Rust crate, CLI, NAPI, WASM, official alias, and verification sections. This documents existing release surfaces only; it does not change compiler semantics, `xtask/src/compat.rs`, conformance classification, or AST/HIR/MIR structures.
- Added the M20 security and supply-chain gate. Root and package npm manifests now carry license metadata for maintained release packages, and `cargo xtask verify-supply-chain` checks `Cargo.lock`, official lock presence, pinned `pnpm@9.0.0`, exact npm dependency versions, native platform package `files` lists, and Cargo metadata resolution.
- Added `docs/SECURITY_SUPPLY_CHAIN.md`, documenting locked inputs, package metadata rules, external advisory audit commands (`cargo audit`, `pnpm audit --prod`), artifact provenance, and compatibility boundary handling for `xtask/src/compat.rs`.
- Added the M20 release-facing architecture document. It summarizes crate ownership, source/tokenizer/AST/HIR/MIR/codegen layering, `AstDocument<K>` arena constraints, target-split MIR documents, public projection requirements, CLI/NAPI/WASM entry points, `xtask/src/compat.rs` boundaries, conformance evidence classification, and release gates.
- Extended `cargo xtask verify-release-docs` so the architecture document is checked for those core sections and vocabulary. This documents existing architecture and release boundaries only; it does not change compiler semantics or AST/HIR/MIR structures.
- Added the M20 conformance report template. It requires report path, command, lock hash, official baselines, runner environment, suite/package/backend scope, `rust-backed` / `mixed` / `shim-backed` totals, file-level coverage, failure classes, `xtask/src/compat.rs` change classification, Rust migration plan for temporary semantic shims, and final acceptance decision.
- Extended `cargo xtask verify-release-docs` so the report template is a release-doc gate, including the coverage labels and compatibility-classification language needed by the project completion criteria.
- Added the M20 crates.io metadata gate. Workspace package metadata now carries repository/homepage/keywords/categories, public compiler crates have descriptions, docs.rs URLs, crate READMEs, and versioned local path dependencies, while internal tooling or npm-distributed binding crates are explicitly `publish = false`.
- Added `cargo xtask verify-crate-metadata`, which runs `cargo metadata` and verifies every workspace package has release metadata plus a non-empty README. For publishable crates it also requires versioned path dependencies so later cargo package/publish dry-runs do not rely on wildcard internal dependency requirements.
- Added the first M20 release documentation skeleton: root `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/COMPATIBILITY_MATRIX.md`, and `docs/RELEASE_CHECKLIST.md`.
- Added README coverage for every current source-controlled `packages/**/package.json` directory, including the NAPI loader package, WASM package, native platform optional packages, and official package-name aliases. Ignored generated wasm-bindgen output directories remain outside this source-documentation gate.
- Added `cargo xtask verify-release-docs`, which checks the release documentation skeleton files are non-empty, every source-controlled package manifest directory has a README, and package `files` arrays explicitly include `README.md` when present.
- This slice is publication/documentation infrastructure only. It does not change compiler semantics, `xtask/src/compat.rs`, conformance classification, or AST/HIR/MIR structures.

## Current Performance / Incremental Slice

- Added the first M19 benchmark framework gate. `cargo xtask bench` writes stable fixtures for Vue 2 template, Vue 3 template, Vue 3 SFC, and Vue 3 SSR under `target/bench/fixtures`, runs the real `vuec` CLI benchmark target for each, and writes `target/bench/bench-report.json`.
- The report records input bytes/hashes, git commit and dirty state, OS/arch, Rust/Node/npm/pnpm versions, lock hash, iterations, elapsed microseconds, and per-iteration timings.
- The gate also prepares locked official npm compiler packages under `target/bench/official-js` from `compat/official-revisions.lock` and records official JS compiler timings for the same fixture set. This is benchmark/reporting infrastructure only; it does not change compiler semantics, AST/HIR/MIR structures, cache behavior, or conformance classification.
- Added an in-memory `vuec_sfc::SfcCompiler` descriptor cache keyed by filename, source hash, and parse mode. `cargo xtask verify-incremental` verifies same-file/same-source descriptor reuse, same-file changed-source invalidation, and Vue 2.7 parse-mode cache reuse.
- `cargo xtask bench` now records best-effort `peakRssBytes` per Rust CLI and official JS compiler child process using `sysinfo` process sampling. The field is nullable for very short-lived processes or unsupported sampling windows, but the current Windows benchmark run records non-null RSS values for representative cases.
- Added `vuec compile-batch`, which compiles independent inputs concurrently with a bounded worker count and returns stable input-order JSON results. The new `cargo xtask verify-parallel` gate builds the real CLI and verifies Vue 2 template, Vue 3 template, Vue 3 SFC, and Vue 3 SSR batch targets.
- Added `vuec_vue3_dom::DomCompiler`, which caches the parsed and DOM-normalized `Vue3Ast` for unchanged template inputs, invalidates changed same-file sources, and separates parse-option cache entries. Transform/codegen still runs on a cloned AST per compile, so cached AST state is not polluted by later transforms.
- Added arena preallocation support in `vuec_ast::AstDocument` and used shared template capacity hints in Vue 3 `base_parse` and Vue 2 public AST projection. The new `cargo xtask verify-arena` gate verifies deterministic `NodeId` allocation, tree invariants, and parser-entry preallocation without changing AST/HIR/MIR structures.
- Added internal JS source-text interning in `vuec_js::JsAstStore`, sharing repeated expression/statement/pattern/program source strings behind the JS side store while keeping `JsEntry.source` serialized as a plain string and AST/HIR/MIR nodes on `JsExprId` / `JsStmtId` / `JsPatternId` handles.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `git diff --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_js -p vuec_vue3_core -p xtask`, `cargo test -p vuec_js --lib`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core --lib`, `cargo test -p xtask`, `cargo xtask verify-string-interning`, `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-dom` (`133/133`), `cargo xtask run-output-contract --version-line vue3 --package @vue/compiler-dom` (`5/5`), and `cargo xtask bench --iterations 1 --skip-official-js` (Rust benchmark cases pass; official JS is pending because it was skipped). Arena slice validation also covered `cargo check -p vuec_ast -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_vue2 -p xtask`, `cargo test -p vuec_ast --lib`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue2 --lib`, `cargo xtask verify-arena`, Vue2/Vue2.7 compiler conformance, and Vue2 output contracts.
- Broader `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue27-sfc` was attempted during this slice, but the current workspace artifacts fail on `vue2_7::vue/compiler-sfc` conformance (`73/144`, with failures concentrated in existing Vue2.7 SFC compileScript/cssVars/stylePluginScoped areas). That suite is outside this arena allocation impact area; the focused Vue2 template, Vue2.7 template, and Vue3 DOM official conformance/output contracts above are the validation evidence for this slice.
- Remaining M19 work: none; optional Vize / Verter / Fervid comparison remains optional.

## Current Vue 2.7 SFC Slice

- Vue 2.7 SFC style compilation now has a Rust style compiler/SFC wrapper path for preprocessing, scoped/CSS-var transforms, maps, and diagnostics, plus a narrow generated JavaScript API adapter for PostCSS plugin callbacks/options. The adapter strips non-serializable PostCSS callback fields from the bridge payload, runs them with the local PostCSS runtime after Rust returns CSS, preserves `rawResult.opts`, records sync-mode async plugin errors, and makes `compileStyleAsync` return a real `Promise`.
- This PostCSS callback execution is API-boundary support, not Rust compiler semantics. Coverage reports classify `compileStyle.spec.ts` as `mixed`; the full `vue27-sfc` suite is therefore top-level `mixed` even though the non-PostCSS SFC files remain `rust-backed`.
- The current scoped/style formatting pass preserves official spaces before rule braces for plain scoped rules, deep selectors, pseudo elements, and nested `@media` / `@supports` blocks.
- Vue 2.7 script-setup codegen now models the upstream `__TEST__` behavior for the internal `__sfc` marker: default public output still emits the production marker, while official conformance aliases pass a hidden bridge option so Rust omits it in test snapshots and preserves the exact empty return spelling `return {  }`. The alias only forwards the test-build signal and hydrates binding metadata; generated code is not string-rewritten in JavaScript.
- Focused official result: `packages/compiler-sfc/test/compileStyle.spec.ts` passes `10/10`; `packages/compiler-sfc/test/stylePluginScoped.spec.ts` passes `5/5`.
- Focused official result: `packages/compiler-sfc/test/cssVars.spec.ts` passes `13/13`; `packages/compiler-sfc/test/compileScript.spec.ts` passes `77/77`.
- Full official result: `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue27-sfc` passes `144/144`, with coverage `mixed: 10/10`, `rust-backed: 134/134`, `shim-backed: 0/0`.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo test -p vuec_sfc --lib`, `cargo test -p vuec_node_bridge`, `cargo test -p xtask`, `cargo test -p vuec_napi`, `cargo test -p vuec_wasm --lib`, `cargo xtask run-output-contract --version-line vue2_7 --package vue --entry vue/compiler-sfc` (`5/5`), `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue27-sfc` (`144/144`), `cargo xtask summarize-compat --locked` (`7/7`), and `git diff --check`.
- Remaining Vue 2.7 SFC work: none in the official Vue 2.7 SFC conformance suite. Progress reports must keep the PostCSS callback path marked `mixed`.

## Current WASM Slice

- Added `crates/vuec_wasm` as the first M17 wasm-bindgen crate. It exposes JSON-string ABI functions for Vue 2 template compile, Vue 3 DOM/SSR compile, SFC parse/template/script/style compile, diagnostics, and source-map carrying result objects while delegating all compiler behavior to existing Rust crates.
- Added the local `@vuec-rs/wasm` package with an async `init()` loader, TypeScript declarations, and a Node smoke. The generated wasm-bindgen directories `packages/wasm/pkg` and `packages/wasm/pkg-node` are ignored and regenerated by verification.
- `vuec_wasm` now initializes the wasm runtime with a wasm32-only `wee_alloc` global allocator and `console_error_panic_hook` installed from a wasm-bindgen start function.
- WASM exported JSON-string functions now convert recoverable ABI errors into JSON: invalid options JSON and serialization failures return `errors` and `diagnostics` arrays with stable `VUEC_WASM_*` codes. The Node smoke imports the raw generated wasm module to verify invalid-options conversion at the ABI boundary.
- `cargo xtask verify-wasm` runs Rust API tests, builds `vuec_wasm` for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, runs `wasm-bindgen --target nodejs`, and executes the Node smoke. `cargo xtask verify-wasm-browser` runs `wasm-pack test --headless --chrome crates/vuec_wasm` and supports `VUEC_WASM_CHROMEDRIVER` when a matching ChromeDriver must be pinned. `cargo xtask verify-wasm-wasi` builds the `wasi_smoke` binary for `wasm32-wasip1`, runs it with wasmtime, and validates stdout JSON.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `git diff --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_wasm -p xtask`, `cargo test -p vuec_wasm -p xtask`, `cargo xtask verify-wasm`, `VUEC_WASM_CHROMEDRIVER=target/wasm-browser/chromedriver-148/chromedriver-win64/chromedriver.exe cargo xtask verify-wasm-browser`, `cargo check -p vuec_wasm --bin wasi_smoke --target wasm32-wasip1`, and `cargo xtask verify-wasm-wasi`.

## Current Vue 3 SSR Public Compile Defaults Slice

- Vue 3 SSR public `compile` now normalizes official defaults in Rust: SSR output is prefixed by default, `cacheHandlers` and `hoistStatic` are disabled even if callers pass them, default/module `scopeId` is preserved, and explicit `mode: "function"` suppresses `scopeId` like official `@vue/compiler-ssr`.
- The bridge forwards whether `mode` was explicitly present so Rust can distinguish default public function-style output from an explicit function-mode request. SFC SSR template calls mark module mode as explicit, preserving scoped SFC SSR output.
- This did not change `xtask/src/compat.rs`, coverage routing, or official SSR conformance classification. `vue3-ssr` remains `mixed` (`129/129`, `rust-backed 0/0`) because the prepared official SSR source suite still executes official TypeScript source plus alias runtime paths. Direct alias probes also still show exact output differences for broader SSR codegen/template-literal formatting, so this is not full SSR parity.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `git diff --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_vue3_ssr -p vuec_sfc -p vuec_node_bridge -p xtask`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core --lib`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_ssr --lib`, `cargo test -p vuec_node_bridge`, `cargo test -p vuec_sfc --lib`, `cargo test -p xtask`, `cargo build -p vuec_node_bridge`, direct official-vs-Rust `@vue/compiler-ssr.compile` alias probes, `cargo xtask verify-npm-alias --version-line vue3 --package @vue/compiler-ssr`, `cargo xtask run-option-matrix --version-line vue3 --package @vue/compiler-ssr`, `cargo xtask run-output-contract --version-line vue3 --package @vue/compiler-ssr`, `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-ssr`, and `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-sfc`.

## Current Vue CLI Slice

- Added `crates/vuec_cli` as the first real `vuec` binary. The CLI delegates compiler behavior to existing Rust compiler crates and keeps command parsing/output formatting separate from compiler semantics.
- Implemented `compile-template` for Vue 2 and Vue 3 templates, `compile-sfc` for Vue 3 SFC descriptors/template/script/style output, `compile-ssr` for template or SFC SSR rendering, `parse-sfc`, `conformance` as an `xtask run-conformance` wrapper, and a smoke `bench` loop.
- CLI outputs human-readable code by default and JSON result objects with `--json`. It can render diagnostics to stderr with `--diagnostics` and write source maps with `--map-out` when the underlying compiler result provides a map.
- Added `cargo xtask verify-cli`, which builds the real `vuec` binary, writes fixture files under `target/cli-smoke`, and verifies help, Vue 2 template JSON, Vue 3 template JSON plus source-map file, diagnostics stderr, Vue 3 SFC JSON, SSR JSON, parse-sfc JSON, and bench JSON. `vuec --help` now exits with code 0 via the shared CLI output path.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `git diff --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_cli -p xtask`, `cargo test -p vuec_cli -p xtask`, and `cargo xtask verify-cli`.

## Current Runtime Smoke Slice

- Added `crates/vuec_runtime_tests` for M15 runtime validation. It compiles representative templates through the Rust Vue 2, Vue 3 DOM, and Vue 3 SSR compiler crates, then executes the generated render functions in Node against the official runtimes already provisioned under `target/compat/npm`.
- Runtime coverage includes Vue 2.6 DOM mount, Vue 2.7 DOM mount, Vue 3 DOM mount, Vue 3 SSR string rendering, Vue 3 SSR runtime HTML comparison against official `@vue/compiler-ssr`, and Vue 3 hydration warning capture through `jsdom`.
- This crate is an execution harness and smoke-test surface, not a compiler semantics layer. It does not edit `xtask/src/compat.rs`, and it does not reclassify official conformance suites.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `git diff --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_runtime_tests`, `cargo test -p vuec_runtime_tests`, `cargo test --workspace`, and `cargo xtask summarize-compat --locked`.

## Completed This Round

- Completed the Vue 3 SFC final compileScript/source-map closure slice.
- `vuec_vue3_core` now accepts argumented `v-on` handler statements such as `throw new Error(\`msg\`);` by falling back from expression parsing to JS program parsing when official inline-handler syntax requires statements. Event-handler source-map collection now includes statement identifiers and allowed globals such as `Error`, closing the inline-template source-map failure without weakening normal directive-expression diagnostics.
- `vuec_node_bridge` now exposes Vue 3 public loc `offset` and `column` values as UTF-16 units while keeping Rust byte ranges for `loc.source` extraction. This makes official SFC `source.slice(loc.start.offset, loc.end.offset)` work for `<script>` and `<script setup>` blocks containing non-BMP characters, closing the module-string import syntax failure with emoji import names.
- No AST/HIR/MIR structure changed, and `xtask/src/compat.rs` was not touched. SFC coverage is still classified as `mixed`, not standalone Rust SFC parity, because official SFC TypeScript source runs through generated Rust aliases.
- Focused prepared official SFC `compileScript.spec.ts -t "imports should support module string names syntax"` and `compileScript.spec.ts -t "inlineTemplate mode source map"` both pass (`1/1` each). Full Vue 3 SFC conformance now passes `461/461` official tests, with coverage `mixed 461/461`, `rust-backed 0/0`, and `shim-backed 0/0`.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `git diff --check`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core --lib` (`243/243`), `cargo test -p vuec_node_bridge -- --nocapture` (`20/20`), focused Rust tests for event-handler source maps and directive diagnostics, `cargo build -p vuec_node_bridge`, `cargo xtask verify-npm-alias --version-line vue3` (`4/4`), focused prepared SFC Vitest for module-string imports and inline-template source maps, and `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-sfc` (`461/461`, mixed coverage).

- Completed the Vue 3 SFC inline-template component `v-model` binding and diagnostic parity slice.
- `vuec_vue3_core` now emits component `v-model` props in exact render output (`modelValue` / `onUpdate:modelValue`, static argument names, and model modifiers), marks component model props as dynamic patch props, collects `_unref` / `_isRef` helpers for setup bindings, and orders inline helper imports to match official SFC snapshots.
- Rust core diagnostics now report official `v-model` errors for missing expressions, invalid member expressions, props, and const/setup-const bindings. DOM diagnostics suppress invalid native-element `v-model` code `58` when binding-expression diagnostics preempt it, matching official transformModel early-return behavior. `xtask/src/compat.rs` only adds official error message adapter data for codes `41`-`45`; no compiler semantics were added there, and AST/HIR/MIR structures did not change.
- Focused prepared SFC `compileScript.spec.ts -t "demote const reactive binding to let when used in v-model (inlineTemplate)|v-model should error on literal const bindings"` now passes (`2/2`), and `compileTemplate.spec.ts -t "template errors"` remains passing (`1/1`).
- Full Vue 3 SFC conformance improves to `458/462` at the suite wrapper level. The classified coverage remains `mixed 458/461` with `3` assertion failures (`imports should support module string names syntax`, `inlineTemplate mode ssr codegen`, `inlineTemplate mode source map`), `rust-backed 0/0`, and `shim-backed 0/0`. Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, focused Rust `base_compile_emits_inline_component_v_model_props_and_diagnostics`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core --lib` (`242/242`), `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_dom --lib` (`25/25`), `cargo build -p vuec_node_bridge`, direct npm/rust-alias diagnostic comparison for representative `v-model` errors, `cargo xtask verify-npm-alias --version-line vue3`, the two focused prepared SFC Vitest runs above, and `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-sfc` (expected failing report, `458/462`, mixed coverage).

- Bridged Vue 3 compiler-sfc prepared `compileTemplate` asset URL options into the Rust DOM alias path.
- The SFC conformance preparer now patches copied official `compileTemplate.ts` so `transformAssetUrls` object / legacy tag configs are forwarded as serializable compiler options to the generated `@vue/compiler-dom` alias, while leaving the existing official node transform setup intact.
- The DOM alias still calls the Rust bridge for public `compile`. This is runner/API adapter support for a non-serializable function-transform option path; it does not add compiler semantics to `xtask/src/compat.rs`, does not change AST/HIR/MIR structures, and does not make mixed SFC conformance count as standalone Rust SFC parity.
- Focused official SFC `compileTemplate.spec.ts` `transform asset url options` now passes. Full Vue 3 DOM conformance remains `133/133`, and full Vue 3 SFC remains `mixed` but improves from `414/461` to `415/461`, with `46` failures still open. `compileTemplate.spec.ts` now has `16/22` passing and `6` failing.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `git diff --check`, `cargo test -p xtask` (`26/26`), `cargo build -p vuec_node_bridge`, prepared Vitest `compileTemplate.spec.ts -t "transform asset url options"` (`1/1`), `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-dom` (`133/133`), and `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-sfc` (expected failing report, `415/461`, `46` failed, mixed coverage).

- Fixed the Vue 3 compiler-sfc prepared Vitest runner's TypeScript transform target by pinning `oxc.target` to `es2020`. This removes the Vitest/Vite/OXC class-field ordering crash around official `ScriptCompileContext.source = this.descriptor.source`.
- Updated the generated Vue 3 `@vue/compiler-dom` alias adapter so compiler-core re-exported utilities forward to the existing `vue3CoreRuntime`, while DOM public bridge entrypoints stay bridged to Rust. The alias also now exposes a DOM `parserOptions` object for official SFC template import-usage analysis.
- Current SFC conformance improved from `96/461` to `384/461` official tests passed, still `mixed` coverage with `77` failures. Focused `compileScript.spec.ts` improved to `70/88`; remaining failures are real downstream SFC/DOM gaps such as inline template compilation, module-string import syntax, defineProps destructure behavior, template asset/srcset transforms, and parse padding/source-location exactness.
- This touched `xtask/src/compat.rs` only for runner transform configuration and API/import adapter behavior. It does not implement compiler semantics in `xtask`, does not change AST/HIR/MIR structure, and does not make mixed SFC conformance count as standalone Rust SFC parity.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `git diff --check`, `cargo test -p xtask` (`24/24`), `cargo xtask diff-api --version-line vue3 --package @vue/compiler-dom`, `cargo xtask verify-npm-alias --version-line vue3`, focused prepared Vitest `compileScript.spec.ts` (`70/88`), `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-dom` (`133/133`), and `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-sfc` (expected failing report, `384/461`, `77` failed, mixed coverage).

- Wired Vue 3 compiler-sfc official conformance to a real prepared Vitest runner.
- The SFC runner now prepares official compiler-sfc tests/source, generated compiler-core relative shims, official DOM `stringifyStatic` source for relative test imports, deterministic SFC runner dependencies, and Vitest aliases for Rust compiler aliases plus SFC npm dependencies. It also classifies Vue 3 SFC conformance as `mixed` and counts Vitest suite-load failures as failures.
- Current SFC conformance is no longer pending/not-wired: `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-sfc` executes `461` tests and reports `96` passed / `365` failed. The current failures expose real downstream gaps in SFC `compileScript` descriptor/source handling, template asset URL output, and parse padding/source-location behavior. This is harness/import-adapter progress only, not Rust SFC parity.
- This touched `xtask/src/compat.rs` only for conformance runner wiring, dependency resolution, import aliases, and report accounting. It does not implement compiler semantics in `xtask`, does not change AST/HIR/MIR structure, and does not make mixed/shim conformance count as Rust compiler parity.

- Added Rust-backed Vue 3 exact render emitter object props merge codegen for public `baseCompile`.
- The exact emitter now emits official-shaped `_mergeProps`, `_normalizeProps`, `_guardReactiveProps`, and `_toHandlers` output for argument-less `v-bind="obj"` and `v-on="listeners"`, including native/component listener differences, source-order merge segments, cached event handler cache-slot sharing, and dynamic bind arg key fallbacks such as `_ctx.key || ""` / `_camelize(_ctx.name || "")`.
- Slot outlets now pass merged props into `_renderSlot(...)` for `<slot v-bind=... v-on=... :foo=...>`, while dynamic `:[name]` no longer gets misclassified as the slot outlet name. Native class/style bindings now use `CLASS` / `STYLE` patch flags and normalizers where official output requires them, while component object spreads keep dynamic prop names.

- Added Rust-backed Vue 3 exact render emitter `v-once` cache-wrapper codegen for public `baseCompile`.
- The exact emitter now emits `_cache[n] || (...)` wrappers with `_setBlockTracking(-1, true)`, `.cacheIndex = n`, `_setBlockTracking(1)`, and final `_cache[n]` return for `v-once` nodes. Plain `v-once` renders vnode-root code, while `v-if` and `v-for` wrap the complete generated branch-chain / render-list fragment expression.
- Cache slot allocation now keeps once wrappers distinct from `v-memo`, `v-for` memo render-list caches, cacheHandlers, and static child caches. Same-node `v-memo v-once` preserves memo-before-once slot order, `v-for v-memo v-once` preserves the render-list memo slot plus reserved transformMemo slot before the once wrapper, and nested once bodies avoid nested block-tracking wrappers while retaining static child caches.

- Root render generation now owns one `MemoIndex` and threads it through element, component slot, dynamic slot, fragment, `v-for`, static vnode chunk, and static child cache rendering. - Added a Rust-backed Vue 3 DOM exact-emitter `stringifyStatic` static HTML slice. `Vue3CompilerOptions.stringify_static` now enables threshold-based `_createStaticVNode(...)` emission for root cached child arrays when `hoist_static` is true.
- The Rust slice covers conservative pure static HTML chunks: native HTML elements, static attrs from a known stringifiable attr subset, static text, official thresholds (`20` total nodes or `5` elements with props), HTML escaping, void-tag handling, and bailouts for runtime directives, slots, non-HTML namespaces, non-stringifiable table-placement tags, `v-once`, interpolations, and dynamic bindings.
- `vuec_node_bridge` now parses `stringifyStatic`, `stringify_static`, and internal `__vuecStringifyStatic`. `xtask/src/compat.rs` projects function-valued `transformHoist` presence to that sentinel for the public DOM bridge; this is bridge/API adapter support for the Rust compiler option, not alias JavaScript semantics.
- This remains exact-emitter DOM target/codegen work. It does not put stringify/cache/helper data into HIR or MIR and does not claim full official `stringifyStatic` parity for constant dynamic bindings, `v-html` / `v-text`, scoped attrs, namespaces, DOM placement edge cases, or asset-import string concatenation.

- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core lower_vue3_ast_to_dom_mir_projects_component_tags_structurally`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core generate_vue3_dom_mir_emits_component_tags_from_mir`, `cargo check -p vuec_ast -p vuec_vue3_core`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core`, and `cargo test -p vuec_ast`.

- Extended HIR and Vue 3 DOM MIR props with ordered segment payloads. `HirProps` and `Vue3DomProps` now preserve static attrs, dynamic bindings, events, object `v-bind`, and object `v-on` in source order, while keeping the existing flat views as derived convenience data.
- DOM lowering now registers dynamic argument expressions and object spread expressions in `JsAstStore` once, projects object spreads / dynamic args to `FULL_PROPS`, and keeps `dynamic_props` limited to static patch-name lists. `Vue3DomProps` carries normalize intent so target codegen no longer infers `_normalizeProps` / `_guardReactiveProps` from the source AST.
- `generate_vue3_dom_mir` now emits `_mergeProps`, `_normalizeProps`, `_guardReactiveProps`, `_toHandlers`, `_toHandlerKey`, dynamic `v-bind` key fallback, object-listener preserve-case mode, and `16 /* FULL_PROPS */` from MIR only.
- This remains structural target-codegen work. Exact Vue 3 DOM/compiler-core parity still needs component asset references in MIR, directive argument dynamism, cacheHandlers event caching, full hoist output, and migration of the exact emitter to complete `Vue3DomMir` consumption.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_ast -p vuec_vue3_core`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core lower_vue3_ast_to_dom_mir_keeps_ordered_prop_segments_and_object_spreads`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core generate_vue3_dom_mir_emits_merge_and_dynamic_props_from_mir`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core`, and `cargo test -p vuec_ast`.

- Expanded `vuec_ast::Vue3VNodeCall` with target-specific `Vue3DomProps` and `Vue3DomDirective` payloads. DOM lowering derives these from HIR props/directive uses, so `JsAstStore` expression ownership stays single-source and MIR codegen no longer needs to read `Vue3Ast` to recover current prop/directive data.
- `generate_vue3_dom_mir` now emits static attrs, dynamic bindings, event props, class normalization, runtime directive wrappers, custom directive declarations, `v-show` helper usage, and the related helper imports from MIR.
- This is still structural target-codegen work. Exact Vue 3 DOM parity needs richer MIR fields for merge props, dynamic arg helper lowering, component asset references, directive arg dynamism, cacheHandlers, full hoist output, and official patch-flag behavior.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_ast -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_node_bridge -p xtask`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core lower_vue3_ast_to_dom_mir_records_hir_mir_edges_and_js_store`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core generate_vue3_dom_mir`, `cargo test -p vuec_ast -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_node_bridge -p xtask`, and `git diff --check`.

- Added `vuec_vue3_core::generate_vue3_dom_mir` as a standalone target-split codegen entry for structural DOM MIR. It emits render code from `Vue3DomMir` plus `JsAstStore` without reading the original AST, and derives module-mode helper imports from MIR node kinds.
- The initial MIR emitter covers the current structural subset: VNode calls, text/interpolation text VNodes, slot outlets, `v-if` comment fallbacks, `v-for` render-list wrappers, fragment arrays, top-level `v-once` cache wrappers, conservative hoist references, patch flags, and dynamic prop names.
- This does not replace the exact AST emitter and does not complete Vue 3 DOM exact codegen. Props/directive payloads that are not yet represented in `Vue3DomMir` remain impossible for this emitter to recover, so the next work is MIR shape expansion and exact DOM codegen parity.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_node_bridge -p xtask`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core generate_vue3_dom_mir`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core`, and `git diff --check`.

- Migrated Vue 3 exact render codegen state selection from legacy AST/code-string probing toward transformed `Vue3Root` fields. `Vue3Dialect::generate` now prefers `Vue3Root.helpers`, `Vue3Root.components`, and `Vue3Root.directives` for helper imports/destructuring and asset declarations, while keeping raw-AST fallback scans for direct `generate(ast, options)` callers that have not run `transform`.
- Component codegen now aligns with root asset collection: built-in components render as runtime helpers, dynamic `<component :is>` renders through `_resolveDynamicComponent(...)`, and only collected component assets receive `_resolveComponent(...)` declarations. Runtime directive codegen now wraps current exact-emitter VNodes with `_withDirectives(...)`, emits custom directive / `v-show` runtime references from root or fallback state, and applies `NEED_PATCH` when runtime directives are present.
- This is exact emitter/root-state migration work, not full DOM MIR codegen completion. `Vue3DomMir` codegen, full official patch-flag parity, and broader compiler-core internal transform/codegen migration remain open.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_node_bridge -p xtask`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core generate_`, focused `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core base_compile_marks_vnode_hook_need_patch`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_node_bridge -p xtask`, and `git diff --check`.

- Moved Vue 3 root asset/helper collection into Rust AST transform state. `Vue3Dialect::transform` now writes `Vue3Root.helpers`, `Vue3Root.components`, and `Vue3Root.directives` while preserving existing `TransformContext.helpers` behavior for the current emitter path.
- The collection records component assets, built-in component helpers, dynamic component resolution helpers, custom runtime directives, `v-show` runtime helper usage, and structural helper needs such as fragments, render lists, memo, comments, slots, text, and class normalization. Structural directives such as `v-if`, `v-for`, `v-once`, and `v-memo` are not reported as runtime directive assets.
- Added focused AST contract tests that verify these root fields through `project_public()`. Exact MIR/codegen consumption of `Vue3Root` asset/helper fields remains a later pipeline migration step.
- Verification this round: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo check -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_node_bridge -p xtask`, `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core -p vuec_node_bridge -p xtask`, and `git diff --check`.

- Extended `vuec_vue3_core::lower_vue3_ast_to_dom_mir` so DOM MIR can project target-level static hoist and `v-once` cache wrappers without leaking hoist/cache indices into HIR.
- Static hoist projection is deliberately conservative: it only wraps all-static native subtrees when `hoist_static` is enabled, skips the single root element, and avoids nested hoist wrappers inside an already hoisted subtree. `v-once` cache projection wraps top-level once nodes with sequential `Vue3DomMirKind::Cache` slots and suppresses nested once wrappers while lowering inside an existing cache.
- This is still structural MIR work. Exact hoist declarations, cacheHandlers event caching, `v-memo` MIR cache shape, official cacheStatic output parity, and DOM MIR codegen remain open.

- Extended DOM and SSR structural lowering so adjacent `v-if` / `v-else-if` / `v-else` siblings lower into one `HirIf` with multiple `HirIfBranch` entries and corresponding target-specific MIR `If` nodes. Branch bodies still lower through the normal element lowering path.
- The lowering now preserves `v-for` priority over branch-chain grouping and avoids double-registering the first branch condition in `JsAstStore`. Exact official branch codegen, key injection, comment carry-over, and runtime output parity remain open.
- Verification this round: `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core lower_vue3_ast_to_dom_mir` and `cargo test -p vuec_vue3_core lower_vue3_ast_to_ssr_mir`.

- `vuec_node_bridge` now forwards deterministic DOM parser metadata and uses the same Rust raw-text / namespace rules in parse diagnostics, avoiding false CDATA, invalid end-tag, missing end-tag, and missing interpolation diagnostics during alias hydration.

- Tightened the Rust-backed Vue 3 parser EOF/error-recovery behavior for official `compiler-core/__tests__/parse.spec.ts`.

## Vue 3 Parser Recovery Parity Round

- Completed official Vue 3 `compiler-core/__tests__/parse.spec.ts` parity through the Rust-backed parser path: `parse.spec.ts` now reports `151/151` passing in the prepared Vue 3 core conformance suite.
- Extended `vuec_html` tokenization for parser-recovery boundaries: invalid-space end tags such as `</ b`, complete/incomplete empty end tags, bogus question tags such as `<?xml?>`, and invalid `=`-prefixed attribute names.
- Updated `vuec_vue3_core::base_parse` to match official AST recovery for invalid end tags, raw-text end tags, bogus question tags, EOF comments, empty interpolation locs, missing dynamic directive argument brackets, and EOF start-tag trailing-solidus recovery.
- Updated `vuec_node_bridge` diagnostics/projection to match official parser error codes, messages, locations, and public AST loc quirks for the completed parse cases.
- `xtask/src/compat.rs` changes are limited to API/test-runner support: official parser error-message hydration and a prepared Vitest `toHaveBeenWarned` matcher. These do not count as compiler semantics.
- Current full Vue 3 compiler-core conformance remains expected-failing at `402/652`; remaining failures are transform/codegen-oriented and should be handled in later Rust-backed transform/codegen slices.

- Added a Vue 3 compiler-core public runtime compatibility layer to generated Rust alias packages: enum objects now carry official numeric/string values, runtime helper exports are stable symbols with `helperNameMap`, `locStub` and source-position utilities exist, and AST builder functions such as `createSimpleExpression`, `createObjectExpression`, `createCallExpression`, `createVNodeCall`, `createRoot`, and cache/conditional/function builders now return official-shaped nodes.
- Added a JS-side transform compatibility core for official compiler-core tests: `createTransformContext`, `transform`, `traverseNode`, `traverseChildren`, `replaceNode`, `removeNode`, `hoist`, `cache`, `helper`, `removeHelper`, structural directive wrapping, minimal `transformIf` / `transformFor` / `transformElement` / `transformText` / `transformSlotOutlet` / directive transforms, and a minimal `generate` implementation.
- Updated the prepared Vue 3 core conformance transform modules so imports such as `../src/transforms/vIf` can access non-top-level internal transform exports through the alias package's non-enumerable `__vuecRuntime`, without adding those names to the public `Object.keys()` API manifest.
- Improved Vue 3 parser public projection: raw directive attributes are now parsed into `Vue3Prop::Directive`, and the Node bridge hydrates `baseParse` / DOM parse results into official-like public AST objects with `helpers` as `Set`, `loc`, `codegenNode: undefined`, element `ns` / `tagType`, directive nodes, and simple-expression interpolation content.
- Current Vue 3 core conformance status moved from the previous loading/export-failure surface to deeper semantic failures: `cargo xtask run-conformance --suite vue3-core` now runs 652 official tests, with 92 passing and 560 failing. Remaining high-volume failures are parser exact AST shape, transformElement/buildProps semantics, v-if/v-for codegen nodes, expression rewriting, and exact codegen snapshots.

## Previous Round

- Added `vuec_node_bridge`, a JSON stdin/stdout bridge binary that lets generated Node alias packages call the current Rust Vue 2, Vue 3 core/dom/ssr, and SFC compiler entry points.

- Replaced `verify-npm-alias` pending output with a real require-and-smoke-call check for all seven compiler targets.
- Regenerated Rust API manifests under `compat/api/rust/`; `cargo xtask export-api --all`, `cargo xtask diff-api --all`, and `cargo xtask verify-npm-alias --all` now pass.
- This bridge is deliberately recorded as a development bridge, not the final NAPI package required by M16. Remaining official conformance and option/output contract work must still exercise real behavior and cannot rely on export shape alone.

## Earlier API Round

- Replaced the spec-only `export-api` behavior with a real Node probe that installs exact official npm package versions from `compat/official-revisions.lock` under `target/compat/npm/<version-line>` and records `Object.keys(require(...)).sort()`, export type details, function arity, class/async flags, package version, type declaration path, require status, lock hash, and official revision.
- Changed `cargo xtask export-api --all` to generate both official and Rust-side manifests. Official manifests pass for all seven compiler targets; Rust manifests are generated as `pending` until alias packages exist under `target/compat/rust-alias/<version-line>`.

## Earlier Vue 2 Round

- Replaced the old `vuec_vue2` skeleton with a recursive Vue 2 element AST that preserves raw attrs, processed attrs/props/directives/events, structural directive state, static flags, slot/component/model metadata, diagnostics spans, and public AST projection.

- Updated `xtask` version-line parsing so the deterministic plan commands accept `vue2_6`, `vue2_7`, and `vue3` directly, while preserving `vue26` / `vue27` compatibility.
- Generated the initial Vue 2.6 `vue-template-compiler` option matrix and output contract files under `compat/`.

- `xtask` now includes compatibility-oriented JSON report commands and the official lock file.

## Notes

- This repository started from docs only; no compiler code existed before the current implementation work.
- The new Vue 3 DOM / SSR / style crates are not official-compatible yet. They establish module boundaries, API shapes, and smoke-tested behavior that must be expanded against official compiler tests.
- The expanded Vue 2 compiler is substantially closer to M09, but official Vue 2.6 parity is not proven until `run-conformance` executes the official compiler tests through the Rust alias/NAPI package.
